Every session at two seeds (form 0 and form 1). For practice sessions, the W1 twin and the W5 fresh look-again of question 1 are shown too.

Session 1 · Wed 2 Dec · The ? in a bond · form 0
  Q1 s1-1 · bond · the whole is asked · W 95 = 37 + 58 · key 95
    Teacher: Look at the bond and its empty circle.
    Read aloud: Look at the bond and its empty circle. The top circle has a question mark. The circles below show 37 and 58.
    Bond: top (?) · parts 37, 58
    1. [decide] Is the ? the whole or a part?
       ✓ The whole
       · A part   ← qAsPart: “The ? is in the top circle.”
       rung 1: Which circle is on top?
       rung 2: The top circle is the whole. The others are parts.
       rung 3: Find the ?. Is it in the top circle?
       rung 4: The ? is the whole.
    2. [find] Now find the ?.
       box: ? = [ ]   key 95
       wrong 21 ← takeWhenWhole (took one part from the other when the whole was asked): “The ? is the whole. It is bigger than each part.”
       wrong 85 ← noCarry (forgot the carry): “Ten ones make one ten. Put it with the tens.”
       wrong 815 ← carryWhole (wrote a place total of ten or more whole): “Each place holds one digit. Ten ones make one ten.”
       wrong 94 ← countStartOn (counted the start number when counting on): “Start counting after the first number.”
       wrong 105 ← extraTen (counted one ten too many): “Check the tens again.”
       rung 1: Which numbers are the parts?
       rung 2: The whole is the two parts put together.
       rung 3: Add the ones first. Then the tens.
       rung 4: The ? is 95. 37 + 58 = 95.

  Q3 s1-3 · bond · a part is asked · W 91 = 65 + 26 · key 26
    Teacher: Look at the bond and its empty circle.
    Read aloud: Look at the bond and its empty circle. The top circle shows 91. The circles below show a question mark and 65.
    Bond: top 91 · parts (?), 65
    1. [decide] Is the ? the whole or a part?
       · The whole   ← qAsWhole: “The ? is in a lower circle. Those are parts.”
       ✓ A part
       rung 1: Which circle is on top?
       rung 2: The top circle is the whole. The others are parts.
       rung 3: Find the ?. Is it in the top circle?
       rung 4: The ? is a part.
    2. [find] Now find the ?.
       box: ? = [ ]   key 26
       wrong 156 ← addWhenPart (added the two numbers when a part was asked): “The ? is a part. A part is smaller than the whole.”
       wrong 16 ← tenOff (took one ten too many): “Check the tens again.”
       rung 1: Which number is the whole?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.
       rung 3: Count up from the part you know to the whole.
       rung 4: The ? is 26. Check: 65 + 26 = 91.
    3. [chips · not graded] How did you find it?
       chips: I counted up · I took away · I added · I just knew

Session 2 · Thu 3 Dec · The box in a number sentence · form 0
  Q1 s2-1 · box · the whole is asked · W 89 = 12 + 77 · key 89 · form ?-b=d
    Teacher: Find the number in the box.
    Read aloud: Find the number in the box. A box take away 12 is 77.
    Sentence: □ − 12 = 77
    1. [decide] Is the box the whole or a part?
       ✓ The whole
       · A part   ← boxAsPart: “Are the two numbers you see both parts?”
       rung 1: Which number is made of the other two?
       rung 2: The whole is what the two parts make.
       rung 3: You start with the whole, then take a part away.
       rung 4: The box is the whole.
    2. [find] Now find the number in the box.
       box: [ ] − 12 = 77   key 89
       bond shown after the tap: top (?) · parts 12, 77
       wrong 65 ← takeWhenWhole (took one part from the other when the whole was asked): “The box is the whole. It is bigger than each part.”
       wrong 88 ← countStartOn (counted the start number when counting on): “Start counting after the first number.”
       wrong 99 ← extraTen (counted one ten too many): “Check the tens again.”
       rung 1: Which numbers are the parts?
       rung 2: The whole is the two parts put together.
       rung 3: Add the ones first. Then the tens.
       rung 4: The box is 89. 12 + 77 = 89.
  Q2 s2-2 · box · a part is asked · W 50 = 39 + 11 · key 11 · form c-?=d
    Teacher: Find the number in the box.
    Read aloud: Find the number in the box. 50 take away a box is 39.
    Sentence: 50 − □ = 39
    1. [decide] Is the box the whole or a part?
       · The whole   ← boxAsWhole: “Is the box made of the other two numbers?”
       ✓ A part

       rung 2: The whole is what the two parts make.
       rung 3: You start with the whole, then take a part away.
       rung 4: The box is a part.
    2. [find] Now find the number in the box.
       box: 50 − [ ] = 39   key 11

       wrong 21 ← noReduce (traded, but did not take one from the next place): “You traded. Did the next place go down by one?”

       wrong 1 ← tenOff (took one ten too many): “Check the tens again.”
       rung 1: Which number is the whole?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.

  Q3 s2-3 · box · the whole is asked · W 81 = 44 + 37 · key 81 · form a+b=?
    Teacher: Find the number in the box.
    Read aloud: Find the number in the box. 44 plus 37 is a box.
    Sentence: 44 + 37 = □
    1. [decide] Is the box the whole or a part?
       ✓ The whole
       · A part   ← boxAsPart: “Are the two numbers you see both parts?”
       rung 1: Which number is made of the other two?
       rung 2: The whole is what the two parts make.
       rung 3: The two numbers being added are the parts.
       rung 4: The box is the whole.
    2. [find] Now find the number in the box.
       box: 44 + 37 = [ ]   key 81
       bond shown after the tap: top (?) · parts 44, 37
       wrong 71 ← noCarry (forgot the carry): “Ten ones make one ten. Put it with the tens.”
       wrong 711 ← carryWhole (wrote a place total of ten or more whole): “Each place holds one digit. Ten ones make one ten.”
       wrong 80 ← countStartOn (counted the start number when counting on): “Start counting after the first number.”
       wrong 91 ← extraTen (counted one ten too many): “Check the tens again.”
       rung 1: Which numbers are the parts?
       rung 2: The whole is the two parts put together.
       rung 3: Add the ones first. Then the tens.
       rung 4: The box is 81. 44 + 37 = 81.

Session 3 · Fri 4 Dec · Read a bar · form 0
  Q1 s3-1 · read · the whole is asked · W 11 = 3 + 8 · key 11
    Teacher: Look at the bar.
    Read aloud: Look at the bar. The line over the bar has a question mark. The bar shows 3 and 8.
    Bar: [ over the bar: ? ]  [ 3 | 8 ]  widths 3:8 (left part 27 %)
    1. [whole] Which is the whole?
       ✓ The ?
       · 3   ← partAsWhole: “That number sits on a part of the bar.”
       · 8   ← partAsWhole: “That number sits on a part of the bar.”
       rung 1: What does the line over the whole bar show?
       rung 2: It goes over both parts. That is the whole.
       rung 3: Tap what is on that line.
       rung 4: The whole is the ?.
    2. [sentence] Which number sentence does the bar say?
       · 8 − 3 = ?   ← takeSeen: “That sentence takes one part from the other.”
       · 8 − ? = 3   ← bigPartWholeTake: “That sentence makes a part the whole.”
       ✓ 3 + 8 = ?
       · 3 + ? = 8   ← bigPartWholeAdd: “That sentence makes a part the whole.”
       rung 1: Look at the bar. Is the ? over the bar, or inside it?
       rung 2: Parts put together make the whole.
       rung 3: Find the sentence that does that.
       rung 4: This sentence fits the bar: 3 + 8 = ?

       · 41   ← partAsWhole: “That number sits on a part of the bar.”
       · The ?   ← qAsWhole: “The ? sits on a part of the bar.”
       rung 1: What does the line over the whole bar show?
       rung 2: It goes over both parts. That is the whole.
       rung 3: Tap what is on that line.
       rung 4: The whole is 88.
    2. [sentence] Which number sentence does the bar say?
       ✓ 88 − 41 = ?
       · ? − 41 = 88   ← qAsWhole: “That sentence makes the ? the whole.”
       · 88 + 41 = ?   ← addSeen: “That sentence adds the whole to a part.”
       · 88 + ? = 41   ← wholeAsPart: “That sentence makes the whole a part.”
       rung 1: Look at the bar. Is the ? over the bar, or inside it?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.
       rung 3: Find the sentence that does that.
       rung 4: This sentence fits the bar: 88 − 41 = ?

Session 4 · Mon 7 Dec · Label a bar: find the whole · form 0
  Q1 s4-1 · labelWhole · the whole is asked · W 18 = 16 + 2 · key 18 · story joinLater · setting class · word more
    Teacher: A class had 16 sheets of paper. The office gave it 2 more sheets. How many sheets does the class have now?
    Read aloud: A class had 16 sheets of paper. The office gave it 2 more sheets. How many sheets does the class have now?
    1. [label] Put the numbers and the ? on the bar.
       empty bar, widths 16:2 · chips 16  2  ?
       key: over the bar ? · parts 16 and 2
       rung 1: What does the question ask for?
       rung 2: The line over the whole bar is the whole. The pieces of the bar are the parts.
       rung 3: The ? goes where the number we must find belongs.
       rung 4: Here is the bar. The ? is the whole. 16 and 2 are the parts.
    2. [sentence] Which number sentence fits your bar?
       · 16 − 2 = ?   ← takeSeen: “That sentence takes one part from the other.”
       · 16 − ? = 2   ← bigPartWholeTake: “That sentence makes a part the whole.”
       ✓ 16 + 2 = ?
       · 2 + ? = 16   ← bigPartWholeAdd: “That sentence makes a part the whole.”
       rung 1: Look at the bar. Is the ? over the bar, or inside it?
       rung 2: Parts put together make the whole.
       rung 3: Find the sentence that does that.
       rung 4: This sentence fits the bar: 16 + 2 = ?
    3. [find] Now type the answer.
       box: [ ] sheets.   key 18
       rung 1: Which numbers are the parts?
       rung 2: The whole is the two parts put together.
       rung 3: Add the ones first. Then the tens.
       rung 4: The answer is 18 sheets. 16 + 2 = 18.

       · 99 + ? = 62   ← wholeAsPart: “That sentence makes the whole a part.”
       ✓ 99 − 62 = ?
       · ? − 62 = 99   ← qAsWhole: “That sentence makes the ? the whole.”
       · 99 + 62 = ?   ← addSeen: “That sentence adds the whole to a part.”
       rung 1: Look at the bar. Is the ? over the bar, or inside it?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.
       rung 3: Find the sentence that does that.
       rung 4: This sentence fits the bar: 99 − 62 = ?
    3. [find] Now type the answer.
       box: [ ] tents.   key 37
       wrong 38 ← countStart (counted the start number as a jump): “Count the jumps, not the number you start on.”
       rung 1: Which number is the whole?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.
       rung 3: Count up from the part you know to the whole.
       rung 4: The answer is 37 tents. 99 − 62 = 37.

       · 65 − 12 = ?   ← takeSeen: “That sentence takes one part from the other.”

  Q1 s7-1 · build · the whole is asked · W 88 = 41 + 47 · key 88 · story joinLater · setting shelf · word more
    Teacher: A shelf had 41 cans. A worker put 47 more cans on it. How many cans are on the shelf now?
    Read aloud: A shelf had 41 cans. A worker put 47 more cans on it. How many cans are on the shelf now?
    1. [shape · warm-up, not counted] Warm-up: which bar fits this story?
       ✓ (drawing) One bar in two parts
       · (drawing) Two bars   ← twoBars: “Two bars are for comparing. Here everything goes in one group.”
       · (drawing) One bar in three parts   ← threeParts: “The whole is not a part. It goes over the bar.”
       rung 1: Is the story about one set or two?
       rung 2: One set split into two parts is one bar.
       rung 3: Find one bar cut into two parts.
       rung 4: One bar in two parts fits. The story is one set.
    2. [label] Put the numbers and the ? on the bar.
       empty bar, widths 41:47 · chips 47  41  ? · self-check after Check
       key: over the bar ? · parts 41 and 47
       rung 1: What does the question ask for?
       rung 2: The line over the whole bar is the whole. The pieces of the bar are the parts.
       rung 3: The ? goes where the number we must find belongs.
       rung 4: Here is the bar. The ? is the whole. 41 and 47 are the parts.
    3. [sentence] Which number sentence fits your bar?
       · 41 + ? = 47   ← bigPartWholeAdd: “That sentence makes a part the whole.”
       ✓ 47 + 41 = ?
       · 47 − ? = 41   ← bigPartWholeTake: “That sentence makes a part the whole.”
       · 47 − 41 = ?   ← takeSeen: “That sentence takes one part from the other.”
       rung 1: Look at the bar. Is the ? over the bar, or inside it?
       rung 2: Parts put together make the whole.
       rung 3: Find the sentence that does that.
       rung 4: This sentence fits the bar: 47 + 41 = ?
    4. [find] Now type the answer.
       box: [ ] cans.   key 88
       wrong 6 ← takeWhenWhole (took one part from the other when the whole was asked): “The ? is the whole. It is bigger than each part.”
       wrong 87 ← countStartOn (counted the start number when counting on): “Start counting after the first number.”
       wrong 98 ← extraTen (counted one ten too many): “Check the tens again.”
       rung 1: Which numbers are the parts?
       rung 2: The whole is the two parts put together.
       rung 3: Add the ones first. Then the tens.
       rung 4: The answer is 88 cans. 41 + 47 = 88.

  Q2 s7-2 · build · a part is asked · W 75 = 43 + 32 · key 32 · story takeAway · setting kit · word left

    Read aloud: A craft kit had 75 beads. A child used 43 beads. How many beads are left in the kit?

       rung 3: The ? goes where the number we must find belongs.
       rung 4: Here is the bar. 75 is the whole. 43 and the ? are the parts.
    2. [sentence] Which number sentence fits your bar?
       · 75 + 43 = ?   ← addSeen: “That sentence adds the whole to a part.”
       ✓ 43 + ? = 75
       · ? − 43 = 75   ← qAsWhole: “That sentence makes the ? the whole.”
       · 75 + ? = 43   ← wholeAsPart: “That sentence makes the whole a part.”

       rung 1: Which number is the whole?

Session 8 · Fri 11 Dec · Build the bar: bigger numbers · form 1
  Q1 s8-1 · build · a part is asked · W 522 = 367 + 155 · key 155 · story startJoin · setting camp · word more
    Teacher: Campers put up 367 more tents at a camp. Now the camp has 522 tents. How many tents were there at first?
    Read aloud: Campers put up 367 more tents at a camp. Now the camp has 522 tents. How many tents were there at first?
    1. [shape · warm-up, not counted] Warm-up: which bar fits this story?
       ✓ (drawing) One bar in two parts
       · (drawing) Two bars   ← twoBars: “Two bars are for comparing. Here everything goes in one group.”
       · (drawing) One bar in three parts   ← threeParts: “The whole is not a part. It goes over the bar.”
       rung 1: Is the story about one set or two?
       rung 2: One set split into two parts is one bar.
       rung 3: Find one bar cut into two parts.
       rung 4: One bar in two parts fits. The story is one set.
    2. [label] Put the numbers and the ? on the bar.
       empty bar, widths 367:155 · chips ?  367  522 · self-check after Check
       key: over the bar 522 · parts 367 and ?
       rung 1: What does the question ask for?
       rung 2: The line over the whole bar is the whole. The pieces of the bar are the parts.
       rung 3: The ? goes where the number we must find belongs.
       rung 4: Here is the bar. 522 is the whole. 367 and the ? are the parts.
    3. [sentence] Which number sentence fits your bar?
       ✓ 522 − 367 = ?
       · 522 + 367 = ?   ← addSeen: “That sentence adds the whole to a part.”
       · 522 + ? = 367   ← wholeAsPart: “That sentence makes the whole a part.”
       · ? − 367 = 522   ← qAsWhole: “That sentence makes the ? the whole.”
       rung 1: Look at the bar. Is the ? over the bar, or inside it?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.
       rung 3: Find the sentence that does that.
       rung 4: This sentence fits the bar: 522 − 367 = ?
    4. [find] Now type the answer.
       box: [ ] tents.   key 155
       wrong 889 ← addWhenPart (added the two numbers when a part was asked): “The ? is a part. A part is smaller than the whole.”
       wrong 245 ← sfl (forgot the trade (took the smaller digit from the larger in each place)): “Look at the ones. Is there enough to take away?”
       wrong 265 ← noReduce (traded, but did not take one from the next place): “You traded. Did the next place go down by one?”
       wrong 156 ← countStart (counted the start number as a jump): “Check the ones again.”
       wrong 145 ← tenOff (took one ten too many): “Check the tens again.”
       rung 1: Which number is the whole?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.
       rung 3: Count up from the part you know to the whole.
       rung 4: The answer is 155 tents. 522 − 367 = 155.

       rung 1: Which number is the whole?

       rung 3: Count up from the part you know to the whole.
       rung 4: The answer is 19 crayons. 40 − 21 = 19.

  Q1 s11-1 · build · a part is asked · W 780 = 464 + 316 · key 316 · story takeAway · setting kit · word left
    Teacher: A craft kit had 780 beads. A child used 464 beads. How many beads are left in the kit?
    Read aloud: A craft kit had 780 beads. A child used 464 beads. How many beads are left in the kit?
    1. [shape · warm-up, not counted] Warm-up: which bar fits this story?
       ✓ (drawing) One bar in two parts
       · (drawing) Two bars   ← twoBars: “Two bars are for comparing. Here everything goes in one group.”
       · (drawing) One bar in three parts   ← threeParts: “The whole is not a part. It goes over the bar.”
       rung 1: Is the story about one set or two?
       rung 2: One set split into two parts is one bar.
       rung 3: Find one bar cut into two parts.
       rung 4: One bar in two parts fits. The story is one set.
    2. [label] Put the numbers and the ? on the bar.
       empty bar, widths 464:316 · chips 780  ?  464 · self-check after Check
       key: over the bar 780 · parts 464 and ?
       rung 1: What does the question ask for?
       rung 2: The line over the whole bar is the whole. The pieces of the bar are the parts.
       rung 3: The ? goes where the number we must find belongs.
       rung 4: Here is the bar. 780 is the whole. 464 and the ? are the parts.
    3. [sentence] Which number sentence fits your bar?
       · ? − 464 = 780   ← qAsWhole: “That sentence makes the ? the whole.”
       ✓ 464 + ? = 780
       · 780 + 464 = ?   ← addSeen: “That sentence adds the whole to a part.”
       · 780 + ? = 464   ← wholeAsPart: “That sentence makes the whole a part.”
       rung 1: Look at the bar. Is the ? over the bar, or inside it?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.
       rung 3: Find the sentence that does that.
       rung 4: This sentence fits the bar: 464 + ? = 780
    4. [find] Now type the answer.
       box: [ ] beads.   key 316
       wrong 324 ← sfl (forgot the trade (took the smaller digit from the larger in each place)): “Look at the ones. Is there enough to take away?”
       wrong 317 ← countStart (counted the start number as a jump): “Check the ones again.”
       wrong 306 ← tenOff (took one ten too many): “Check the tens again.”
       rung 1: Which number is the whole?
       rung 2: A part is the whole take away the other part.
       rung 3: Count up from the part you know to the whole.
       rung 4: The answer is 316 beads. 780 − 464 = 316.
